# aterm-agent: spec recovery from unit tests

Source: `crates/aterm-agent` (read only). The tests were read in nine chunks, together with the code each
one reaches; nothing was compiled or run, and every claim comes from reading the code. The chunk manifest
said 2712 tests. A `#[test]` count over the crate today finds 2840: the nine chunks read 2758 of them
(several manifest counts were short by the stand-in bodies and late tests they found), and six files that
landed after the chunks were cut were not read (§3).

Result (recount 2026-11-04): **646 specs**, retiring **2360 tests** as their primary spec. **77
chunk-6 groups** (statements pending) hold **293 more**. **10 tests are not spec samples, or must be
fixed first** (§4). The crate now holds 8299 `#[test]` functions, so most of it has no row (§3).

## 0. Notation used in the statements

- Function names are module-qualified under `crates/aterm-agent/src` (`harness::…`, `supervise::…`, `drive_cli::…`, or the crate root).
- `⊓`: the meet of two policies; `⊖`: saturating `−`; `sat(x)`: saturating `i64`.
- **Writes** are the `key`, `send`, `paste` and `turn` requests; **act requests** add `signal`. **Fenced(w, J)**: w carries `if=row_guard(row)` for its row as drawn in the judged read J, plus `if-gen=g(J)` where the host's help names it and `if-human=n(J)` where it publishes a person count.
- A **read** is a `text --json` reply, parsed by `screen::parse_text_json` (supervise) or `upgrade_drive::parse_screen` (harness); its **live zone** is the rows from aterm-phase's `busy_zone_start` / `transcript_end` on.
- **Transcript rows** are JSONL; a **main-chain** row has `isSidechain != true`; an **answer** is a main-chain assistant row with a real model id.
- **St, Row, Facts, Step**: `upgrade_drive::St` (the per-conversation upgrade record), its owner-facing projection `upgrade_status::Row`, and the reducer's `upgrade::{Facts,Step}`.
- **Dir(agent)**: the agent's Claude directory as Claude Code resolves it (`HOME/.claude` when `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is unset; absolute as given; relative or empty against the directory the agent started in).
- **Inv_bounds(s)**: every map of the accounts `State` within its MAX, every key admissible, counts saturating.
- **Power order ≤ on `SupervisorConfig`**: `a ≤ b` iff `a.limits(b) ∨ a = b`, per key. Text keys are incomparable "own" values; the opt-in keys (`limit_reset`, `usage_notes`, `usage_credits`) default false, the bottom; `usage_tap`, `stall_term` and `stuck_break_note` default true, so their off is a limit; `limit_reset_kinds` moves only with `limit_reset` off.
- **ShellSem**: a reference model, to be written in Clean, of zsh 5.9 (`-f`) and bash for the fragment the classifier judges; **ReadSet**: the (program, argv-form) pairs the classifier's tables call reads.
- A **refinement** row binds the real functions to the named `ty_model!` machine under the projection its conformance test computes: every real decision is an action the model enables at the projected state, every model invariant holds there, and every `Buggy`/knob variant is caught.
- A spec whose functions touch fs, processes, sockets, threads or the clock is stated over the oracle the code injects (closure, trait object, host trait); where it injects none, the row carries gap 8.
- `Session::{supervise, supervise_to, watch, await_turn, await_turn_to}` and `run::render_result` are `#[cfg(test)]` seams over the hosted loop's engine; a row naming them states the engine's behaviour through them.

## 1. Specs (TSV)

Columns: `id`; `kind`; `functions`; `statement`; `blocking_gaps`, the Trust gaps that block its proof, numbered as in the gap table of `docs/TRUST-LANE.md` (Blocking gaps) (§3 names the gaps that block every row); `subsumes`, `p/s`: the tests whose §2 `primary` is this spec / all tests that sample it (`0/0`: a lemma, implied by tests other specs retire).

align-capture-bounded	contract	harness::align::{capture_bounded,capture_bounded_until,capture_bounded_owned,capture_bounded_owned_watching,capture_with,CaptureOwner,kill_capture_child,read_capture_pipe,read_capture_pipe_until}	Ordinary capture completes stdout and observes exit before returning its capped bytes and actual code. Owned capture preserves the starter tether and stale-executable refusal; its observed natural exit records Reaped before cancelling, draining available bytes and joining stdout, and signals no remaining group member. Unix return and unwind settle the actual child and join any started reader; a cancel pipe prevents an escaped stdout holder from retaining the reader. Deadline or stop ends work, not ownership: an uncertain owning wait retains the caller stack with paced retries, and ECHILD permanently removes numeric signal/wait authority without releasing custody. Owned end attempts retain birth-checked TERM/KILL grace before group signaling and owning reap; neither end nor group-signal success is descendant-quiescence evidence. Watched text on stdout or Unix stderr retains Stopped semantics; external terminal watches remain Unix-only, and capture_bounded has no stop predicate. A past cell is this tab's week as read on this Mac: the last reading at or before the cell's end in the same window, never interpolated, blank before the first reading and across an epoch, `↺` in the cell a reset falls in. A future cell shows one glyph by priority `╳` > `→` > `↺` > `$` > `┄` > level: `╳` where a stop covers at least half of it, and a stop that covers no cell that much takes the cell holding its start, so every stop takes a cell; every stop the tail says is drawn, a wall the pane waits at now as a stop from `▕` to the vendor reset it stands to (over `┄` where nothing is projected) and the stop both branches of a lean meet in the working pass as in the week pass; `→` in the cell holding each hand-off (the first future cell for a carrier that is not this tab's), one a gap displaces moved to the first cell after the gap, the target's name after it only where the run to the next mark holds the name and one cell more (a lean's `→name?` its `?` too); `↺` at a reset of the drawn account; `$` under credits; `┄` past unknown_from; else the drawn account's fill at the cell's end, linear between Pass.fills' knots. level(x) = clamp(round(x·8/100), 1, 7), and 8 only at 1000 (a reading of 100 or a projected fill), so 63% and 98% are levels 5 and 7; a measured cell is U+2580 + level, a projected one a one-eighth line (`▁`, U+1FB7B − (level − 2), `▔`). The lead's figure, the last past cell, the curve at `▕` and, at this tab's reset, the curve and the tail's `~N%` tell one figure: the chain's first run on this tab's account is shifted to the footer's own figure until its first drop or hand-off, clamped under a fill. A reset further ahead than a week and a day (accounts::believed_reset) is neither said nor drawn. Line E's QuotaLine::ticks are its chart's (lead 0). The chart draws what is left, so every level above is drawn as 9 − it: 63% used is level 4, 98% level 2, a fresh week the top (`▔` projected, `▇` read), a fill or a reading of 100 the floor (`▁`).	1,3,7	15/17

The full lead names every named account with its figure, then ` +N` for the folded rows, or ` used` with one account alone; fold says this tab's and `+N` of every other; this tab's says its own alone. T0 is phrase 1 full · phrase 2 full, T1 both short, T2 phrase 2 short alone (phrase 1 short with no phrase 2); groups are two blanks apart. Over every room from 0 to 200: a laid row fits its room and every wider room lays one; (1) this tab's name and figure never fold; (2) a stop, wall, lean or credits phrase (Phrase::kept) stays, at least short, while any figure shows; (3) no figure shows without its name; (4) each narrower room's parts are a subset of the wider's (names, phrases, a full phrase only where the wider's is full) and its chart is never finer; (5) no rung is finer than 12 cells a day. `↺` and every warn glyph are laid bold (heavy), and this tab's name and figure. 2026-11-07 (glass S8, `docs/DESIGN-harness-glass-rainbow-2026-11-05.md` §2): a new row, stating the S3 code and its tests as they stand. 2026-11-08 (glass cases (a), (b) and (d) pinned, `docs/DESIGN-harness-glass-rainbow-2026-11-05.md` §1.3, §2.4): the design's mocks lay their rungs at 166, 96 and 66 cells, each row cell for cell as §1.3 draws it, with the next rung up not fitting: case (a) R1 (133 cells), R3 (93; R2 takes 101) and R6 (56; R5 takes 72); case (b) R2 (140; R1 takes 172), R5 (94; R4 takes 99) and R7 (64; R6 takes 78), the midnights' ticks of R2 in its chart's cells 2, 11, 19, 27, 35, 43, 51 and 59; case (c) R2, R5 and R7 (135; R1 takes 167); case (d) its full words at all three (64 cells), short from 63 cells to 24, nothing at 23.	1,3,7	6/6
quota-view-words	contract	harness::quota_view::Outlook::quota (Tail, title, spoken), harness::quota_view::QuotaView::{fallback,title_with}, harness::outlook::Figure::{words,spoken}, harness::decimal::whole_used, harness::tap::pct_of, harness::accounts::WindowFig::whole, harness::account_series::Fig::whole, harness::usage::{AccountView::add_reading,hud_line}, harness::account_stats::{window_value,left}, harness::accounts::sample_window, harness::outlook::whole, harness::left::{exact,at_most,ahead,of_whole}	A figure is said as what is left (owner 2026-11-09, "left everywhere"; harness::left): `37% left` (read within 15 minutes, 100 less its use half up), `≤30% left` (an older reading's lower bound of use, 100 less it rounded down; spoken `at most 30 percent left`), `out` (a wall standing on the account, whatever the footer's own figure says) or `?` (unread); `0% left` only for a reading of 100, and a curve short of a fill at least `~1% left`; every percentage a glass word says (the row at every room, the title, the spoken words, the fallback, a column's title) says `left`, except a pace, which says `used a day`, and none names the 5-hour or 7-day window by its short word (a 5-hour stop's title says `a 5-hour limit`). Phrase 1 is `~N% left at ↺ <reset>` (short `↺ <reset>`), left out only where a wait ends at this tab's own reset; phrase 2 is the verdict: a stop `╳ ~<from>–<reset>` or `╳ ~<from>–~<when>` (short `╳ ~<from>`), `╳ from ~<from>` where nothing ends it, `⧗ until <the vendor reset>` for a wait at a wall standing now (its reset exactly, whichever window walled), `? <row> unread from ~<when>`, `$ ~<when>`, a refused pace's short words on the carrier's tab alone (not kept), and none for continuous work or a wall the work moves off, whose title says `the work moves to <row> at the turn's end`. The tap's whole field (tap::pct_of), accounts::WindowFig::whole and account_series::Fig::whole round the tenths half up but give 100 only at 1000 tenths or more (harness::decimal::whole_used); a persisted format-v1 tap's exact tenths override its older rounded whole. Thus the usage line and account-stats JSON say `99%` for 99.96 or a held 999, never `100%`. 2026-11-08 (glass land fixes): account_stats::percent's whole percent is whole_used's too, so `accounts history`'s day lines (cli_history::pct) say a 999-tenths peak `99%` (the stats table says tenths whenever any figure has one, so its whole figures were whole already); the CLI's outlook text says its projections and paces through outlook::whole, which is whole_used's at most 1000, so a projection short of a fill reads at most `~99% by reset`; and a sample note_reading wrote from a held figure is that figure whichever whole it says (whole_used's or the half-up whole a store wrote before it), at the same reset and no later, so a store's 996 is never read back as a new reading of 1000 (accounts::sample_window). 2026-11-08 (case (c) at 170): phrase 1's full form is its short one where no run of this tab's account holds its reset short of a fill, as when the account fills or is left before it: in case (c) the rotation comes back to main once every account is low and main fills ~Sat 04:00, before its reset Sat 10:00, so R2's T0 says `↺ Sat 10:00 · ╳ ~Sat 05:00–Sat 10:00`. 2026-11-08 (case (b) at 170): case (b)'s phrase 1 is `~99% by ↺ Sat 10:00`, its week's 98.875% at its reset whole half up, and its verdict `╳ ~Thu 12 Nov 04:00–Sat 14 Nov 10:00`, the next week's fill at Thu 12 Nov 04:17 said at the hour's grain of a start a day or more ahead; QuotaView::sample (a test seam other crates' tests draw) says case (b)'s replayed words: its tail, title, spoken words and fallback forms. 2026-11-08 (the first reading, glass §2.2): where the view's lead is the footer's own figure (`own`) of a row the replay read nothing of, the reading came since that replay, and the replay's refusal for want of it (`Refusal::NoFigure`, `no reading` on the carrier's tab) is said nowhere: not as phrase 2 at any rung, not in the title and not aloud; the past holds no step and the future is `┄` until a replay reads it. Any other refusal is said as before. 2026-11-09 (owner, "left everywhere"): every form above that says use (`63%`, `≥70%`, `100%`, `~99% by`) now prints as what is left by harness::left (`37% left`, `≤30% left`, `0% left`, `~1% left at`); a wall stays `out`, since it is the vendor's refusal and not a reading; the rounding rules are those of the use it mirrors, so a figure said left and the vendor's own `% used` add to 100.	1,3,7	18/29

footer-segments-order	contract	footer::{segments,fact_segments,text_width,fact_spans,glyph_fact}	segments = present of [MODEL(+effort), WALL, ACCOUNT, PATH, BRANCH, CONTEXT, USAGE] in that order, absent ones omitted, every mark width 1, a fact the person hid shown as its greyed stub; the WALL is `⧗ until <its reset words>` alone (session_usage::wall_text: no window name, no rotation or recovery words); the ACCOUNT unit, present only while line E is not the glass's quota row (FooterFacts::line_e), is account_unit's widest form: `⎆` and the roster label (or `this login`) lit, then its parts attached (no mark, no gap of their own) in their tones, the figure, `· ↺ <reset>` and the short verdict where the quota view has them; no email, week bar, 5-hour part or outlook words; an attached segment never moves a later fact's cells out of their FactKind. 2026-11-07 (glass S8): restated as the code stands since S5 (`docs/DESIGN-harness-glass-rainbow-2026-11-05.md` §1.2, §5.1): the upgrade note left the facts, the account unit moved before the model and became the quota view's words-only fallback, and the row now names the context fact. 2026-11-07 (glass review fixes, §4.1, §4.3, §8 item 16): the WALL's words are a warning, Tone::Warn and bold (Segment::bold), the one fact on line D a person can act on; its `⧗` keeps the marks' ink. The account unit (account_unit: ⎆, then its name, figure, ↺ reset and verdict, each attached in its tone; none while line E is the quota row) narrows as one, by its own forms widest first and only after context, usage, branch and path have given way, the model and the wall beside every form; it is never shown in part; then it goes, then the wall (its floor, walled_floor, is the model with its effort then the wall), then the effort, leaving the model alone: the model is the last fact to give way, every rung with the wall holds the model and effort before it, and a dropped effort never returns; while a wall stands the controls take only rungs that keep it, a wall without the controls outranks the controls with or without the model, and past the wall's rungs, wall or none, the controls outlast the model only beside a unit or a subscription; chips_room reserves the model, the wall and the unit's narrowest form that still says a figure (where that leaves the controls three cells), else the wall's floor; with no unit, the wall's floor where it fits, else the model unless under a subscription, else nothing. settings-permission-default	contract	claude_settings::{permission_default,permission_default_under,Resolved,PermissionDefault}	permission_default(sources), sources in Claude Code's precedence, the highest first: mode = Resolved::Mode{mode, source} for the first document whose `permissions.defaultMode` is a string lights::Mode::of_setting names, source that document; Resolved::Unset where every document is read and none names one; Resolved::Unread(why) where a document at or above the winner is there and cannot be read or parsed, names a policy helper, names a word of_setting does not know, holds a non-string `permissions.defaultMode`, or holds a `defaultMode` key anywhere but its own top-level `permissions`; a document below the winner never changes the answer; bypass_disabled = the first readable document, at any precedence, whose `permissions.disableBypassPermissionsMode` is `disable`. permission_default_under orders the documents: the server-managed copy (`remote-settings.json` in the configuration directory), the managed preference domains (never parsed), the managed folder's drop-ins from the last name to the first and then its file, then sources_under's chain with no managed folder (`--settings` values, the project's local settings, the project's, the user's); no `--permission-mode` or bypass flag on the line is read (`docs/DESIGN-yolo-binary-2026-11-04.md` §3).	3,7	2/2

netstate-schedule	contract	netwatch::NetState::{next_probe,landing,begin,finish,path,moved,owed_at,path_landing}	next_probe = None with nobody waiting or a probe in flight, else min(the owed probe's due time while a path's return owes one, the cadence's: now with no fresh measure, else m.at + (Cadence::steady = STEADY_CADENCE (60 s) after an Up, Cadence::gap = GAP_CADENCE (3 s) after a Down or Unknown that no fresh measure preceded or that was the owed probe, DOWN_CADENCE[min(misses, 3) − 1] (5, 10, then 15 s) after any other Down or Unknown)); path(satisfied) counts only a change: a loss sets lost = now and drops an owed probe, a return after a reading that could not carry traffic (never the first reading) owes one probe due max(now + Cadence::settle = PATH_SETTLE (2 s), last measure + Cadence::gap), a later return moving it, and returns whether one is now owed; moved(now) (the routes moved, whether the path can carry traffic unchanged or unread) owes the same probe, due max(now + Cadence::settle, last measure + Cadence::gap), a later move moving it, except while the path reads lost or no lease lives (then nothing, and false), touches neither the path's reading nor lost, and returns whether one is now owed; begin refuses a second flight; finish takes the owed probe iff its flight began at or after the due time, and counts misses in a row (a Down or an Unknown, an overrun among them, the one that ended a gap or was the owed probe not among them) and an Up resets them; the measure continues the verdict's run (since) when it says the verdict's outcome, else begins one at now; finish returns the Edge iff the verdict's outcome changed (from = the verdict's outcome before, None when nothing stood; to = after; held = now − the run's since before, zero when nothing stood; took = now − begin, zero with none in flight), else None, so with no path reading iff the fresh outcome changed; landing is always later than now: with a probe in flight min(began + budget, now + REACH_POLL) while that end is ahead, else now + REACH_POLL; with none, None while no lease lives, else next_probe when later than now, else now + REACH_POLL, and while the path is lost no later than now + LOST_POLL (its return owes a probe, so a measured wait meets that probe's due time, not the ladder's rung); path_landing (the path-only verdict's next look) is always later than now: now + LOST_POLL (DOWN_CADENCE[0], 5 s) while the path is lost and the loss is younger than LOST_CAP (60 min) on both clocks, the return + Cadence::settle while that is ahead, else None (no loss, settled, or a loss past LOST_CAP).	2,3,5	8/10

A wall its transcript dates (`dated`: a login wall, a usage limit, each look of the model's dated walls taken as both) that has stood its bound (upgrade::LOGIN_WALL_S, upgrade::LIMIT_WALL_S) with nobody seen (`aged`) moves as an idle point does; one nothing dates reads no age and never ages. Real work is told with St::deadline_at stamped only for a line the host took whole (a line cut, refused after its keys or unanswered names none and counts as none, the hold starting again; an untyped turn changes nothing; an older build's spent lines with no deadline are none; a newer build keeps it, another process's is none of this one's), a look that breaks the hold voids it (St::void_deadline: a person, a wall, nothing running), and past it the round signals at a break and names what ran (St::ended_work). owner-rule-rev	contract	supervise::occupancy::owner_rules::{OwnerTables::{from_doc,rule,source,rev},OwnerWhen::{parse,canonical},CwdGlob::{parse,matches,canonical},canonical,rev,rendering,RuleState::of,Snapshot::{of,still_current}}	OwnerTables::from_doc(doc) reads `[harness.occupancy_source.<name>]` and `[[harness.occupancy_rule]]` (at most 8 of each) from the root [harness] table alone, by the grammar, charsets and caps of the occupancy design's §3.3; a refused field drops its own source or rule with one note naming the key and never its value, and a rule that names a source not read is dropped with it. rev(rule, sources, resolved) is SHA-256 of canonical(rule, sources, resolved): sorted `key=value` lines over every field of the rule, of the source it names and of that source's resolved stored URL, every default written out and an optional field not given (scope, path, a URL not resolved yet) writing no line, so each field edited alone changes it, no value reads as a field's absence, and key order and whitespace do not; a rule's `when` joins at most 8 terms and a `hold>=` span is at most 48 h. RuleState::of(rev, confirmed, mode) is Live only when the rule's whole current rev is among the confirmed revs and its mode is live, DryRun when confirmed and dry-run, else Unconfirmed; Snapshot::still_current holds only while the tables in force give the snapshot's rule the same rev. rendering prints the parsed form alone (closed words, validated names, the rule's checked text), never the raw `when`; CwdGlob matches its folder and below, `*` within one component, `~` the home.	1,3,5,7,12	12/14

An instant before the last good read is taken as that read's. restore(name, target, persisted, now) re-bases a book kept for another target (target_of: FNV-1a over the kind, the resolved stored URL, ref and path, or the program; none for an unresolved remote), clamps every stamp past now to now, drops an episode that cannot be one (no move, a first edge after its last, or a latest value the last read did not see), keeps the book while its last good read is within STALE_MS (24 h) of now, so a move made while aterm was down is an edge at the next read, and re-bases it past that; SourcesFile::read refuses a file over 64 KiB or one it cannot read and drops a name not declared. owner_episode_key(rule, episode) is FNV-1a over both, one key per rule and source episode.	1,2,3,5	13/13

A walk that is cut proves nothing: a folder that cannot be listed or lies where macOS never lets the check list (never_listed), more than 20,000 entries (TREE_ENTRIES), a link or path that cannot be resolved, a Windows host, or a disk that does not answer within the look's bound, each unproven; nothing at the path reads nothing, except a git grep word that may name a revision (Tree::revision: with no -- and no --no-index, an operand holding no *, ?, [ or \, or holding ^{ or @{, may_name_revision), which git then reads from its object store (MEASURED 2026-11-05 with git 2.56.0: git grep token src2, src2 a branch and no path, printed the branch's a/.ssh/id_rsa; with a folder src2 beside it git refused the word as both). %(raw:size) is its size alone, %(RAW) no atom); and grep with --cached by any prefix, a revision before its --, or, with no --, an operand holding a :. A path the line builds at run time (an operand holding an expansion or a substitution: "$VAR", $(…), $(<list); since v11 an awk program naming ARGV or a getline whose < takes other than one string literal alone, awk_names_files_at_run_time) and a relative path after a move line_bases cannot follow (cd -, a stack entry, popd, an expanded or globbed target, another user's home, two operands, a relative cd a loop may repeat), on a line whose commands may stand in more than STANDS_MAX (64) directories (each relative target is joined to every directory before it, so a line of distinct ones doubles them: MEASURED 2026-10-29 on the merged tree, a box of 22 distinct cds grew one check past 3 GB in 90 s before the bound; distinct drops a repeat), or with the cwd unknown, and a git grep pathspec read from a directory the check cannot place (descends), refuse as unproven, and since v13 so do a tree a walk cannot prove (listed) and a git read of the object store or index no word bounds (object_read). a .zshenv's PATH or HOME, which nearly every one names and which pick the programs a line runs, the configuration git loads and the home jq and python load from, so refusing them would send every box to a person; a variable Claude Code sets in its own process after launch past its settings' env, which no probe sees; a home spelled through a link the session's cwds do not take, as home_loads_unwritten compares them as written (Claude Code records each cwd as the kernel resolves it, and a macOS home is no link); and git's own configuration (a credential in a remote URL, an http.extraHeader), left as refusing the commonest git reads would send them to a person; each operand (operand_comps: the word, a ~ lead resolved to the home, an expansion lead read as the home at either separator, a root-relative \… also on a drive home's drive) and each home rule are read by path_readings, a hit on any reading a hit, so each reading past the plain / split only adds refusals (stricter only, so no id moves): split at / alone and at \ too (Windows' separator, which Git Bash's MSYS layer also takes), a drive path (C:\a, C:/a) as Git Bash's /c/a spells it, and a rooted path's .. kept as a component (a symlink before it leads elsewhere) and also taken lexically; so a Unix home's rules and a Windows home's (C:\Users\name) match those spellings of a path under it; and a rooted path led by an alias the OS keeps of the root is also read without it (root_alias_free: macOS's /System/Volumes/Data and a /Volumes/<name> volume, MEASURED 2026-10-29 on macOS 27.0.1 to be the home's own inode, the boot volume's entry a link to /, and its lookup prefixes /.nofollow/ and /.resolve/<n>/, MEASURED 2026-10-30 on macOS 27.0.1 to be the home's own inode too (each case-sensitive there, compared case-folded anyway, stricter only); Linux's /proc/<pid>/root, a thread's /proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/… read as its process's, REASONED from proc(5); Git Bash's /proc/cygdrive/<drive>/…, MEASURED 2026-10-30 in the Windows lane (its cat opens it, and not /cygdrive/<drive>/…), and Cygwin's /cygdrive/<drive>/…, REASONED, each as the drive path; a directory holding one of them, /.nofollow, /.resolve, /System, /Volumes, /proc or a process's, read as the root for a recursive reader), and a read by a handle no name rule sees is unproven (handle_read: macOS's volfs /.vol/<device>/<inode>/…, MEASURED the same day to open the home by its numbers, and Linux's /proc/<pid>/cwd, fd and map_files, a thread's under /proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/ too; a relative path spelled so is refused too, stricter only). Stricter only, so no id moves. Every name is compared case-folded (same_name, folded and fold_char: lower, upper, then lower again, with an ASCII fast path; in secret_hit, glob_tokens, component_meets and basename_meets), as a case-insensitive volume opens it: MEASURED 2026-10-28 on this Mac's APFS, cat opens .ſecrets/t, Keychains spelled with the Kelvin sign, .claude.jſon, .paßword-store and .conﬁg as the ASCII names they fold to, and keeps .confıg and .İconfig apart from .config; the fold also takes ı to i, a refusal APFS would not need (stricter only, so no id moves). A name in another Unicode normalization is outside it: the default rules spell ASCII letters, digits and . _ -, which no other canonical spelling reaches but the Kelvin sign's K, which the fold takes. On a Windows host (cfg windows, or a drive home) windows_reach reads Windows' own spellings of each name too (windows_spellings): a name without its :stream suffix and its trailing dots and spaces, and a short 8.3 name whose stem holds a whole basis and no extension, read as that basis (SHORT leads it), held to every long name Windows may have made it of (short_meets, since 2026-10-30, in component_meets and basename_meets: every space and dot dropped, the leading ones among them, each of + , ; = [ ] and a character the OEM code page lacks put as _, upper-cased, REASONED from FAT's and NTFS's short-name rules: SSH~1 of .ssh, PEM~1 of .pem, which *.pem names; MEASURED 2026-10-30 in the Windows lane, dir /x lists ALWU~1 for Al Wu and A_B~1 for a+b and Git Bash's cat opens both, and a home so named had its ALWU~1 and A_B~1 spellings approved by the reading of a stem as the long name itself, with and without a leading dot, which this replaces); an 8.3 name that may stand for any long name (short_name, its shape counted in characters and its whole basis under eight bytes: a stem filled to eight characters, a hashed one-character basis, X and four hex digits, or an extension: CLAUDE~1.JSO, SECRET~1, S1A2B~1, and so a git word such as master~1) and a UNC or device path (unc: \\?\C:\…, \\localhost\C$\…, //?/C:/…, and the NT namespace's \??\C:\… and /??/C:/…), as an operand or as a directory a relative one is read from, are unproven, and a drive-relative path (C:name) is read as relative from each directory the line may stand in and from its drive's own directory, which the check cannot place (its root among them, through the root's aliases: C:'Documents and Settings/<owner>/.claude.json'). MEASURED 2026-10-28 in the Windows lane (d5d51d9544: Windows 11 build 26300 arm64, 8.3 names generated on C:, user lane, Git Bash's MSYS 3.6.10): Git Bash's cat opens CLAUDE~1.JSO, SECRET~1/t, SSH~1/config and CARGO~1/credentials (relative, C:\…\ and /tmp/… spellings), \\?\C:\…, \\.\C:\… and //?/C:/…, and none of a trailing dot or space, a ::$DATA or ::$INDEX_ALLOCATION suffix, or C:name; \\localhost\C$\… and \\127.0.0.1\c$\… are denied to that standard user (an administrator's share); a native reader run from Git Bash (findstr, CreateFileW, as rg, jq and Git for Windows read) opens a last name's trailing dot or space, an intermediate name's trailing dot, a directory's ::$INDEX_ALLOCATION, an 8.3 name, \\?\C:\… and C:.claude.json, and not a file's ::$DATA or an intermediate name's trailing space; the check reads every one of these spellings either way. attention-ownership	invariant	Session::{reconcile,escalate,attention_text,unset_if_ours,finish_supervisor_attention,metadata_requires_proof,metadata_authority,retry_supervisor_attention}	At start a stale own badge is cleared or adopted; other owners and bare attention are untouched. A handed point creates one logical escalation intent, distinct from its last acknowledged entry: refused or uncertain SETs and CLEARs retain custody for bounded, paced metadata retries on an unchanged point. Managed writes verify an existing lease without renewing or acquiring one; missing proof defers, a successor or deliberate handback retires local custody, and handover leaves native badges for the successor. Explicitly unclaimed calls and hosts without lease support from the start retain ordinary legacy behavior, but gain no timer authority; once lease support is confirmed, refusal or release cannot downgrade custody to that fallback. A session's route stands while its connection lives. A mid-call I/O error is a lost reply that drops that connection and its routes, and the next call dials again (re-reading the graph entry); connect failures are lost; unsupported framings fail without I/O. turn-end-decision	contract	policy::turn_end::{decide_turn_end,says_done,clean_defense,open_work_suffix,repo_work_suffix,own_repo_work_suffix,reply_mark}, policy::land_check, policy::needs, TurnEndState::{observe,acted,repeats,restarted,weighs_tools,reads_turn_whole}, ScreenReader::{turn_ran_tools,reads_tool_rows,turn_opens_above}, Session::turn_end_reading, upgrade::transcript_tasked, PriorityWord::prose_rule_id	At an idle turn end under Cruise or Land (never under Off, never into a taskless session), no current prompt asks for the token, and a `Nothing left:` defense, a legacy ATERM-TASK-DONE line or a bare DONE reply is a done claim (says_done, read from those lines alone: no done phrase, idle report or echo of a retired check is one) that ends nothing by its words. CRUISE ends no task: a done claim ⇒ the defend-and-find-more prompt (continue-done-check@v1), suffixed with the open work the claim's own words name (open_work_suffix) and the work the host's probe finds in the session repository that no remote main has (TurnEndReading::repo, own_repo_work_suffix: land what of it the agent made and leave the rest exactly as it is, since the probe reads other sessions' stashes and worktrees too; Land's nudge asks for all of it, repo_work_suffix); otherwise keep going under continue@v1, at most once per point. LAND: the episode's first free point ⇒ the land instruction (land@v1); every free point after it ⇒ the land nudge (land-nudge@v1), with the same suffixes and no count of its own (only the same-reply hold below stops it); a question ⇒ the land answer (land-answer@v1); a land act's yield, a wall's retry of one included, whose reply is a `Nothing left:` defense over a repository the probe finds nothing in (a need or a cut defense included) ⇒ the land check (land-check@v1, land_check::land_check_text), once per defense and a new one only after new work or a new reply; the word reader (clean_defense, open_work_strict) only picks the check's hint clause (land_check::hint_clause) and no longer lands or nudges on its own; the task is landed (done, nothing typed until someone else's turn) only at the check's yield, on a reply whose body is `LANDED` alone (land_check::land_check_answer), over a repository the probe, read again there, finds nothing in; a reply of NEEDS YOU lines alone is a quiet rest (nothing typed until someone else's turn); anything else is the agent's list of what is left, which the next nudge quotes (land_check::listed_suffix), and the same list given again holds, quoted. LAND'S CONTRADICTION (v6 §14.4's 4c): a land act's yield whose reply carries a `Nothing left:` defense line (needs::defense_line) while the probe finds work no remote main has, or, where no land check is owed (the check outranks it over a clean read: an audited defense said again, or a repository the probe could not read), while the agent's own list stands (not at the check's own answer), gets the nudge in its contradiction's words (still land-nudge@v1, land_contradicted): LAND_CONTRADICTED_HEAD, then git's finding (CONTRADICTED_GIT) or the list quoted within CONTRADICTED_QUOTE_UNITS, the one ask to land it and never delete work, and Land's own close (NO_REPOSITORY included), never a way out; every reader of a typed Land act knows its head (is_land_words, approvals::delivers_land, upgrade::is_land_act, stats::opens_with_land_words). In both modes the short streak's back-off paces every act (a done claim lengthens it; a managed worker's first MANAGED_SHORT_TURNS go at once; a turn of real work ends the streak whatever act it answered, a wall's taken retry included). THE SAME-REPLY HOLD (TurnEndState::repeats) covers EVERY act at a turn end: a continuation, a Land nudge, a wall's retry, a limit's resumption, a command's owed continuation and a reset's own continuation. At a point whose reply came again nothing is typed and nothing waited for. A reply (reply_mark) is the turn's earlier and last words, or a read wall's kind and message, each word with a digit in it one number (a countdown, a clock with its `AM` or `PM`, an error id); words no reader reads are one reply; words cut at the screen's top are read whole from the history at every point while a reply is kept (reads_turn_whole), so a later point compares them as its answer read them. The streak keeps the newest REPLIES_KEPT replies (TurnEndState::replies) that acts' answers seen taken (a busy spell, a submit, a redraw) settled to, each with its answers and, at a read wall, its tries: the answers to the wall's own acts (its retry or resumption, a command's owed continuation), never the act's whose answer first met it. An answer counts when it ran no tool (TurnEndReading::ran_tools: turn_ran_tools of the readers whose reads_tool_rows is true, none read for the others; where the turn's opening message is above the screen (turn_opens_above) at a point where it weighs (weighs_tools: an act's answer read, or a reply kept), the loop reads the whole turn from the history above it, or the history to its start (all of the turn the terminal holds), and judges that, and where it reads neither the answer may have run one and is work. Claude Code: a call's `⏺ Tool(…)` row, or one drawn with no `(…)` and its `⎿` output under it (TaskStop's `⏺ Stop Task`, an edit of the plan file's `⏺ Updated plan`), or a collapsed group's `⏺ ` or two-space row read by every clause of 2.1.268's group renderer (clauses joined by `, `, the first capitalised), the first the renderer's and any one a tool's, before the expand hint: `Thought for <time>` (no tool), and the tool clauses edited or editing N files (an `+a -r` after), made N edits, committed, amended commit or cherry-picked <shas>, pushed to <refs>, merged or rebased onto <ref>, a PR or MR operation, one of the renderer's verbs (created, edited, merged, commented on, closed, reopened, marked ready, marked draft, enabled or disabled auto-merge on) before `PR #n` or `MR !n` (`Created PR #12`, never `Waiting on PR #12`), published, searched for N patterns, read N files, listed N directories, REPL'd N times, called <servers>[ N times], ran N agents or `ran agent · <description>`, a lone agent's description whose first word 2.1.268's verb rules conjugate (its tables and rules, UWt: `Reviewed the diff`, `Reviewing the diff…`, `Re-ran the suite`) in a row of at most 500 characters and no count after that word (a hooks summary's `Ran 2 stop hooks` is none), called N tools, ran N shell commands, recalled or wrote N memories, searched memories, the counted and bare clauses each in its -ing form too (`Reading`, `Searching`, `Listing`, `Running`, `Editing`, `Making`, `REPL'ing`, `Calling`, `Recalling`, `Writing`, `Publishing`, `Thinking for`), the git and PR clauses past only, as the binary draws them; with no hint every clause the renderer's and each count's noun a file, pattern, directory, shell command, agent, tool or memory, singular or plural (the git clauses as wait_bg17.out drew them, `  Pushed to main, rebased onto origin/main`), a two-space one standing alone under a blank row or the screen's top (under another group it adds nothing), never a reply's next row nor a row a `⏺ ` or two-space line above wraps onto (that line's, read once from its first row, so the read is linear in the turn's rows), and one whose tool clauses are git or PR ones alone only by what a sentence lacks, wherever it stands: a commit's sha of 7 to 40 hex digits, a ref with a `/`, a PR or MR number after the renderer's verb, or a second clause (a reply's closing `  Committed abc1234, pushed to main` is work; a lone `  Pushed to main`, `  Merged cleanly` or `  Waiting on PR #12` is words); a stop hook's summary and `Ran N PreToolUse hooks` are none, and so is ExitPlanMode's `⏺ Exited plan mode`, a plan handed to a person; or a background task's notice of its end, which opens the program's own turn (task_notice, each end 2.1.268's notice builders draw: `⏺ Background command "…"` completed[ (exit code N)], failed[ with exit code N] or was stopped[ because the system is running low on memory]; `⏺ Monitor "…"` ended without producing output[ (exit N)], stream ended, script failed[ (exit N)] or stopped; `⏺ Agent "…"` finished, is waiting on its own background work, failed: <error>, was stopped[ by Claude| by user] or stopped at its N-turn limit (…); `⏺ Dynamic workflow "…"` completed, failed: <error> or was stopped; `⏺ Remote task "…"` completed successfully, failed, is blocked[: …] or was stopped; then the row's ` · <time>` and ` · delivered to Claude as a <Tool> result (N chars)` parts, the description wrapped or not), never a Monitor's event (`⏺ Monitor event: …`, its monitor running on). agy: a `●` or `○` row that is no reply and no `Thought for` row. Muse Code: a `◆ ` tool head (tool_row), or one ran_row reads (drawn with the ` · ctrl+o` expander, a Monitor or Stopped terminal row with its outcome, a Scheduled cron or Unscheduled id row), or a two-space Read or Searched row) and was busy under min_work. One that ran none counts however long it was busy at a read wall, for every reader whose walls are read, Grok's and Kimi's whose tool rows are unread included (the unit test a_long_answer_at_a_read_wall_is_no_work_whether_or_not_its_reader_reads_tool_rows binds it), and, where tool rows are read, when it says a kept reply again: it is no work (the program's own retries, Muse Code's provider attempts) and neither ends the streak nor restarts the wall's ladder; elsewhere a long answer is work, and where tool rows are read, in new words, it is kept too. So, by what the screen shows, a long answer at a wall no reader names is work where the reader reads neither tool rows nor words (Grok, Kimi and the generic program, whose replies all mark the same: holding or counting it would hold a worker's every long turn), and a long tool-less answer in new words is work where tool rows are read: neither is held, counted in the run below or backed off (a done claim's back-off aside), each paced by its own busy spell of min_work or more. A wall read at the answer is settled by the next point's read; an act whose row stands unanswered leaves the replies (no model action reaches it, since the walk draws nothing while an act is pending: the unit test an_act_whose_row_stands_unanswered_past_its_take_leaves_the_replies_though_the_screen_drew binds it); a turn the books say ran that no loop timed is work; the ledger's point and act rows carry the replies across loops, with the reset the newest answer counted showed (`reset at <unix ms>`), so a loop that takes over at a reset that moves forward holds where the one before held while the wall's placed reset is remembered (MEMORY_TTL past it; past that a reset that names a clock alone is placed anew, a new wall to that loop, one resume per such takeover). A reply no reader names as a wall holds once two answers settled to it, any kept reply said again included (two wordings by turns hold), the generic program's too, whose reader reads no words: holding is the safe side. A read wall holds once it came twice and FUTILE_AFTER_TRIES tries were spent at it (its kept tries or its track's attempts, so a loop that takes over holds where the one before held): a retry ladder keeps its tries and no more, a retryable server error's (5xx, overload) included. A read wall whose date or clock reset is still ahead is waited out while its tries last, and whatever its tries where that reset is not the one its last answer showed (a new reset is a new wall); one whose reset is read relative to its notice (agy's `retryDelay:45s`, `resets in 1h`) is waited out while its tries last. The tab's exception says so (Exception::repeats; a usage, spend or model limit's Exception::limit_repeats, futile, where its notice left only while that limit's track stands). A hold lasts until the reply changes (another wall message included), a date or clock reset is named or passes since the last answer, the one the last answer showed (read again within SAME_RESET, the loop placing a reset on its clock at each read) only while the wall's tries last (passing keeps every walled reply kept with no answers and with its tries, so a reset that moves forward with each answer is resumed FUTILE_AFTER_TRIES times, two wordings of the wall by turns (Muse Code's one-line and two-line usage walls) until one of them meets its last, then held under a futile exception until the wall's message changes to one no kept reply has, the agent works or a person's turn comes; a relative reset, restated ahead of every answer, lifts the same-reply hold only while the wall's tries last, never the bound below, and passing clears nothing, so its answers count as the wall's tries), the host measures the route back up since the last answer (Reach::Up at an Unreachable or CutOff API error: one more try), someone else's turn runs (a person's keys since the last point read and the last answer counted (TurnEndReading::person), or, for Claude Code alone, whose screen names each turn's prompt (TurnEndReading::answered, the `❯` row above its last message), a prompt row other than the last one read; a person's turn lifts the hold only where a point saw that turn busy: one the books alone show, a person's keys since the last point read, lifts nothing unless a tool row of it was read (on the screen, or in the history read to its start), one opening above a screen whose history was not read included (TurnEndReading::tools_unread: an act's answer there may have run one and is work, but this turn is the person's), whatever else it drew and whoever's prompt it answered, in a loop or at a takeover, since the person drives and the hold lifts at their next turn a point sees): one more act goes, a wall's spent tries left one short of FUTILE_AFTER_TRIES; the program's own no-work turn while a reply is kept, a Monitor's event or its own retry, counts into the replies and the run as an act's answer does (one no loop saw busy, its turn's words other than the last point's, whatever wall either showed, and its reply other than the newest kept, counts as a turn of no length where it ran no tool and ends the replies where it ran one; a wall's notice leaving changes no words and is none), a long one that ran no tool at a read wall or saying a kept reply again where tool rows are read included, so saying it again holds and new words count toward the bound below, though a long one ends the short streak; the other programs' readers name no prompt, so a lead agent's message typed into one of them through `aterm ctl send|turn`, which moves no person's keys, and its no-work answer count as that program's own turn), the agent works, a restart is made, Autopilot is parked Off (TurnEndState::park_off; the ledger's off-stood row ends the replies for a loop that follows), the program was started again since the books, or a loop's first point shows other than the newest reply the loop before it kept with no act of that loop's awaited (the program moved while no loop watched); time alone ends nothing, so a loop that takes over days later at the screen it was held at holds. A wording-blind bound stands beside it: NO_WORK_ACTS acts in a row answered by no work (Replies::run, the no-work answers seen taken and the program's own no-work turns; work, someone else's turn, a date or clock reset passing or a restart clears it, and the ledger's replies note carries it as `N in a row`) hold the next point the same way while it shows the newest reply kept (TurnEndState::repeats compares the screen's reply_mark, or the wall a track stands for, with it, as for the same-reply hold), under the same stalled exception and lifted as that hold is (a relative reset aside), so a program rotating through more than REPLIES_KEPT wordings is held, except at a read wall whose date or clock reset passes between its answers: each passing starts the run afresh, so there each wording's own tries bound the resumes, up to REPLIES_KEPT wordings by turns, and more than that are resumed at each reset (none measured draws more than two); NO_WORK_ACTS is 2×REPLIES_KEPT, two answers through each reply the streak keeps, so a wording said again holds first and a loop doing work never reaches it. A read wall's own tries still come first: at one whose tries are not spent the bound is NO_WORK_ACTS + FUTILE_AFTER_TRIES, so a wall met at the end of the run keeps its ladder and read walls of other classes by turns, whose tracks start their tries afresh, are held all the same. codex-alab supervises itself beside this loop (claim.rs), so this loop types nothing into it; a NEEDS YOU line never holds an act, which carries NEEDS_ACK once per new set; a person's short exchange ⇒ left; idle compaction after nothing to type ⇒ nothing; a question under Cruise ⇒ the session word's text (one bare meta first) or an escalation. LAND'S LEFT LEG (docs/DESIGN-leftover-processes-2026-10-16.md): the agent's left rows the host read at the yield (TurnEndReading::leftover, IdleHost::leftover, asked only where the repository is read, fresh at each yield) hold Land while one is neither ended nor kept by a `keep <token>` line under a defense of the episode (needs::kept_tokens, read where needs::defense_body reads, which leaves them out, a NEEDS YOU block's lines keeping nothing; `keep:` and a token copied with its `end=` keep; LandEpisode::kept, the kept tokens the yield's rows no longer list let go, carried in Carried::land) and the episode's left holds are fewer than LEFT_HOLDS, each yield counted once (LandEpisode::left_counted: a yield a back-off reads again before its act takes its hold back first, TurnEndState::uncount_left; an act typed or a turn run since settles it, TurnEndState::settle_left; the books a loop that follows seeds leave an unsettled hold out, TurnEndState::land_books, so that loop counts it where it judges the yield again and it still holds): a `Nothing left:` defense then owes no check and gets Land's nudge with the left suffix (left_suffix, else left_suffix_short, fitted in the room the repository's ask leaves, never pushing it out), the check's `LANDED` lands nothing and checks no defense, and land_held names HoldLeg::Left with its count; a check's `LANDED` the leg held, judged again by a loop that follows with no left row holding it, over a repository read clean, lands; a view that could not say, none asked, an episode whose defenses kept more running rows than KEPT_TOKENS (a new keep token no row read names goes first; Once custody and every later gate pass, the dialog takes the key, never the composer, and the line stands held for the read after it; without read proof custody holds the key even though nothing settles the line under the dialog; every idle point and break the host is offered says it is held (IdlePoint::line_held; custody_holds_host first settles a line whose program ended), so the host types nothing else into the agent there (its carry-on, with no step taken, so the carry-on's hold on the loop's act still runs out on its bound, and the disk-free ask stay owed) and holds the upgrade's step whole while the line stands (LineHeld::Stands: it may still come, or the loop judged it lost and its own restart in place for it stands asked, Restarts::standing, or was refused and the loss is not yet weighed again by Line::lose); once the loop judged it lost and makes no restart for it (LineHeld::Lost) the upgrade's step goes, notice or tell included, as its move ends the process; nothing is badged, noted to the host or asked: one `SKIPPED … held until the worker draws it` per change of what holds it. It settles only by evidence: its draw (whole, a prefix, or joined; entered once as its writer's), a person's keys drawn after it (a key count past the write's), a turn read running after it (Line::ran, set by Line::took through Session::took) once `status` reads the input clear on a read showing none of it, or its program's end (another process named: a restart that took, an upgrade's move, its own end). A read that cannot say what the composer holds (a dialog over it, no composer drawn, a glyph's cell unread) is no evidence either way: the look settles only a draw a break kept, and a turn read running is kept (Line::turned's Turned::Stands, no status read for it: Line::weighs) for the next read that can say: every look at a point weighs it (custody_look: a new point's, a point decided again, a handed point's moved read, the point a spell ends on, the gate's) and so does a break, so a line drawn under a dialog is handed back to its writer once the dialog goes and entered once, never settled with the turn and taken for a person's draft, and one dropped under it goes with the kept turn at the first point after the dialog whose read can say, before the host is offered that point and whatever the point then decides. A move, a new point, an epoch, a thaw, an edge, or a busy spell with no turn read running in it (break reads alone, a frame being drawn) settles nothing. The point a busy spell ended on looks first (custody_after_work): a draw there, in the spell's turn or at a break of it (kept as Line::drawn, so a line drawn at a break and cleared by the point settles drawn, never lost), is handed back to the LeftLine and OwnLine it was written as, so its Enter is its writer's act, never a person's draft. `status input=clear` after the write proves the line read (Held::Read, sticky). input-lost-restart@v1), its `restarting it in place` NOTE written once, and the point resumed writes it once; the host's success word (`adopted`, `relaunched`, `continued`, `done`) settles it Ended and books the restart made only where the process the restart's report names after it (IdleHost::restart_left), else the one the host names (Session::host_process), is another than the one it was written to, or none is named at either end (Restarts::answered, Line::restarted's Restarted::Ended); with the process it was written to named (Restarted::Stands) nothing shows the restart ended it: it stands held, nothing is written to that process, and the restart stands asked (Restarts::deferred), asked again on its back-off as a deferral is, its WAITING step saying `no evidence it ended the process the line was written to`; LandReceipt::of(Gone(p)) = leg Gone, repo p, no head, ref or branch; branch is set only on OnMain. line() appends `branch=<b>` only where b is non-empty, and of_line(line()) = the receipt, a line with no branch= token reading branch empty (9b's journals). land-done-mark-checked	contract	supervise::task_mark::publish_done, harness::land_receipt::{valid_checked_word,CHECKED_WORD_BYTES}, harness::result::{note_checked,checked_of,with_checked,answer,Reread::{write,answer}}	At a landing's done mark with a receipt r, the loop marks `task mark done why=landed checked=<checked_word(r)> …` and notes the same word in the result's sidecar (first line + ` checked=<word>`), so `result`'s OK header ends ` checked=<word>`, and a reread keeps it; with no receipt (codex-alab's, a restated pre-receipt landing) neither carries checked=. note_checked and task records share valid_checked_word: a leg word or a canonical encoded nonempty ref after a 12-digit lowercase head, bounded by CHECKED_WORD_BYTES (derived from the probe's printable character bound, UTF-8 width and percent expansion); malformed escapes, invalid UTF-8 and encoded controls are refused. An ERR reply is kept unchanged; an unnoted sidecar write is appended to the TASK journal note. A failed mark or an ACK not confirming done why=landed leaves publication retryable. Where the landing's yield asked the host for the agent's left rows (TurnEndState::landed_left), the mark adds `left=<word>` (LeftWord::word: `0`, `kept:<n>`, `<n>`, `unchecked`); a replay of a tape recorded without `host.leftover` adds none.	1,7,8	2/2
agent-identity-law	contract	harness::agent_image::{proof,proof_among,in_vendor_layout,claude_native,vendor_team,standins,standins_in,FileId::{of,of_entry},Image::at,Act::of,Act::word,Proof::word}	proof_among(agent, image, places, standins) = Layout iff in_vendor_layout(agent, image, places): for Claude Code, the entry of image.exe's file name in places.claude_versions is a version-named regular file (lstat: never a link) whose (dev, ino) is image's mapped file (image.file, else the file at image.exe), or an atpkg store build of places.roots registered as agent's whose store entry is the mapped file; for codex-alab, an ALab package (alab_bytes); for agy and Muse Code, a registered store build; for Grok Build, Kimi Code and Generic, never. Else Team iff vendor_team(agent) = Some(t) ∧ image.team = Some(t) (Claude Q6L2SF6YDW, agy EQHXZ8M8AV, Muse V9WTTPBFK9; codex-alab, Grok, Kimi: none), where the host gives image.team only for a signature the kernel validated as Developer ID whose running code meets the requirement that its leaf certificate names t (aterm-gui `agent_act::live_team`). Else Seam iff the mapped file or image.script ∈ standins by identity. Else None. No argument is the process's argv[0]. FileId::{of,of_entry} is the file's (dev, ino) on unix and its (volume serial, file index) on Windows, and None where neither is read, so nothing confirms by identity there. An image unlinked since its exec has no layout entry that is its mapped file, so off macOS (no team) it is observed: the law's verdict for what nothing proves. Act::of(p) = On iff p ≠ None; Act words = [on, observe], Proof words = [layout, team, seam]. standins() = standins_in(ATERM_DEV_AGENT_STANDINS) (absolute paths only, at most STANDINS_MAX, links followed) in a build with debug assertions, [] in any other.	1,8	6/6
observe-one-gate	contract	supervise::run::Session::call, supervise::observe::{agent_act,observing,observe_refuses,refusal,refused,drives_agent,OBSERVE_REASON,RESTART_WITHHELD}, supervise::approval_loop::decide_box, supervise::turn_end_loop::decide_to_act_now, supervise::stall::Session::{stall_remedy,withhold_stall_restart}, supervise::beside::Session::{beside_memory_remedy_at,withhold_memory_restart}, supervise::setup::SessionSetup::confirms_agent, supervise::tape::ctl::{TapedCtl,ReplayCtl}::agent_act, harness::agent_image::{Lane,Lane::exempt,withholds,observe_refused,LANE_OPTION,OBSERVE_REFUSAL,WITHHELD}, harness::upgrade_drive::{lane_words,St::lane,type_line,checked_turn_for_duty,take_back,agy::type_text,muse::type_text}, harness::disk::type_resume	With setup.confirms_agent, Session::call(r) for every r with claim::is_input(r) (key, send, turn, paste, feed-bin) or drives_agent(r) (restart, signal: a restart ends the agent and the host types its relaunch line and continuation) asks ctl.agent_act(sid) (Observe for a loop that names no session) after the stop and claim checks, and on Observe answers refusal() (code 1, `aterm-ctl: ` + agent_image::OBSERVE_REFUSAL, `ERR busy supervisor_act=observe …`, never `busy sink`, naming no `supervisor=` holder; observe::refused and agent_image::observe_refused read it, and the server's own) and reaches no transport; every other request (reads, waits, attention and log writes) passes unasked; without confirms_agent nothing is asked (On). Under Observe, decide_box = Escalate{OBSERVE_REASON} for every box; a turn-end act that drives the agent (Type, TypeCommand, Submit, Confirm, Cancel, and Restart, whose relaunch the host types through its own connection as the exempt relaunch lane) becomes Nothing with trace check `agent-observed` before any input reaches the gate, and an Escalate stands. The loop's other restarts read observing() before they send `restart` and, observed, send nothing (no gate refusal either): stall_remedy, with stall_term on and past its bound, says one `STALL-WARNED … ended=no (RESTART_WITHHELD)` and informs the host once an episode (stall_termed), and beside_memory_remedy_at says one `RESTART-REFUSED … reply=(RESTART_WITHHELD)` once per leader (beside_memory_ended, memory_refused); a refusal observe::refused reads in their reply is taken alike. Over every path that restarts (the turn end's Restart, the stall remedy, the memory ceiling beside a self-claim) an observed agent gets 0 restarts and 0 gate refusals, a confirmed one and today's name-only admission 1 each. Outside the loop, every write the window harness makes into a session goes through the control server's input admission, which judges the write and not only its connection (the window harness's own, or any that carries a lane's name, such as the token beside the socket harness_client falls back to when the window's token is refused) and refuses it while the host has not confirmed the agent (aterm-gui `host-observe-one-gate`) unless the write names (`harness-lane=` = LANE_OPTION, sent where the host's `help <verb>` names it: lane_words) a lane THE RULING of 2026-10-27 exempts, Lane::exempt = {Relaunch, Upgrade, CodexAlab, Agy, Muse}: withholds(act, lane) = act = Some(Observe) ∧ ¬(lane is Some(l) ∧ l.exempt()), so a write that names no lane is withheld. St::lane names a record's writes: CodexAlab, Agy, Muse by its agent, and for Claude Code Upgrade when is_upgrade, else Relaunch; the write primitives every lane types with (type_line's send and Enter, checked_turn_for_duty's turn under turn_fenced, type_fenced and type_fenced_for_duty: the relaunch line through type_relaunch_line_* and type_at_prompt, its continuation through type_continuation) and the agy and Muse Code drives' own turns (agy::type_text, muse::type_text) each name their lane on the wire, so into an observed agent an exempt lane's write is taken and every other lane's refused, and St::lane is exempt for every agent and cause. take_back's backspaces, which take a line the host stopped after its keys back out of the composer, name the lane that typed the line (the stopped turn's own; Lane::Upgrade for St::left_typed, which only the upgrade's lines record), so the gate takes them where it took the line and refuses them where it would have refused it. The disk resume (`disk-resume@v1`) and the disk-free ask (`disk-free@v1`) name Lane::DiskResume and Lane::DiskFree, which are not exempt: disk::type_resume answers WITHHELD (`withheld:unconfirmed`, nothing typed, the write never retried) where the reply is observe_refused, `typed` for a confirmed agent. relaunch::launched picks which program a relaunch or a worker runs (by name, layout or record), never whether the harness may type, and upgrade_drive's native-root reads choose an upgrade's source, not an agent's identity. Over every write path of the E2E-14 trace (approval, decline, trust and launch folder-trust dialogs, question, usage-limit, autocompact and auto-mode dialogs, login dialog, survey 0 and its backspace, turn submit, /usage probe, Cruise carry, woken Esc, wall /compact and /login), an observed loop sends no input verb and escalates each box, while On and today's name-only admission each write the row's writes. The act crosses a tape at `ctl.agent-act` as one byte (0 On, 1 Observe), and confirms_agent is written to a tape's setup only when true.	1,3,8	13/13
self-claimed-record-survey-facts	contract	harness::self_claimed::{parse,read_record_facts,read_record_goal,RecordFacts}	parse(t).background_terminals = Some(n) iff t's `backgroundTerminals` is a JSON integer 0 ≤ n ≤ u32::MAX, else None; that key never makes parse refuse a record it would take without it. read_record_facts(h,p,t) is the record at record_path(h,p)'s {goal, background_terminals} iff it parses Present with pid = p, exit = None and sessionId = t, else both None; read_record_goal = read_record_facts(..).goal.	1,3,7,8	2/2
tape-steps-recovery	refinement	supervise::tape::frame::{TapeWriter::{start,append,close,truncate,put},frame_bytes,FRAME_RAW_MAX}, supervise::tape::recover::{recover,salvage,read,unframed,magic_tail}	Rests on astream claim `wire.steps.torn-vs-fault`. A tape is MAGIC (frame format 3), then each frame as one write of one astream step record whose step is its place (the header's 0) and whose body is its kind, its raw length and its payload (LZ4 only where that saves an eighth). recover(b) decodes exactly the records `scan_steps_from(b[8..], 0)` keeps, holding at most HELD_MAX (else TooLarge): a cut anywhere in the last frame, or a byte of it damaged, is Torn at that frame's start with every frame before it read, except astream's nested-frame case: where the last frame's stored bytes hold a whole astream frame of their own (a raw payload whose items carry one), a cut past that frame's last byte is `Steps(LengthPastEnd)` and damage to it `Steps(CorruptFrame)`, a Fault at the last frame, as a rotted length is (the error runs only from torn to fault); a byte damaged before the last frame, a frame repeated, dropped or moved (`Steps(OutOfOrder)`), a whole body that is not what it claims (Unreadable), a magic one byte off before a record the scan keeps (`DamagedMagic`, the byte named) or the magic of another frame format before none (`Format(n)`) is a Fault that recover refuses whole and salvage keeps the frames before; no fault reads as Torn. An item encoded past FRAME_RAW_MAX = MAX_PAYLOAD_LEN − STEP_HEADER − 5 ends the tape at `Mark::Truncated`, and one of exactly FRAME_RAW_MAX is a frame of its own. Refines supervise_tape_recovery_model (SuperviseTapeRecovery) under conformance_tests::project, its TearNested the nested-frame cut (Recover refuses, NestedRefused; a salvage keeps every whole frame before the torn one, NestedKept) and its Rot damage with a whole astream frame after it, a nested one included (so damage to a last frame that nests one, outside the nested frame, is Rot and refused, and RotLast is damage that leaves no whole frame after it), which the bind's two- and three-frame tapes meet through a File read in a raw frame that embeds `encode_step` output, the two-frame tape's last; a CRC-blind reader (Buggy 1), a place-blind one (Buggy 6), one that reads a fault as torn (Buggy 7 on the nested cut) and a salvage that refuses whole (Buggy 8) are caught, and the lax scan they are built on equals astream's with every check on.	1,2,3,5,7,10,12	23/23
tape-replay-divergence	contract	supervise::tape::replay::ReplayCore::{step,mono,clock,exchange,emit,file,consumed,diverged}	Rests on astream claim `effects.cooperative.record-replay`. A strict replay's doors answer only from the tape's records, in order, through astream-effects' `ReplaySeam`: an asked effect whose kind, door (a file read's path) and request are the next record's is served its recorded reply (an output must equal the recorded one); any other (a kind, a door, a path, a request or an output that differs, or an ask past the tape) is astream's `ReplayDivergence` (KindMismatch, DoorDiverged, PathDiverged, RequestDiverged, OutputDiverged, Exhausted), latched as the replay's divergence at that record's ordinal, after which nothing is served.	1,2,5,12	7/7
probe-keyed-index-equals-view	equivalence	supervise::policy::probe::{KeyedProbe::{at,answer,misses},TapeIndex}, supervise::tape::acts::ActsCursor::keyed	Rests on astream claim `effects.keyed-view`. A KeyedProbe standing after seen records answers a (door, request) with `KeyedIndex::lookup(door, request, seen)`: the latest recorded Exchange of that pair at an ordinal strictly before seen, which equals `KeyedView::lookup` over the same records; a pair with none is a miss (named in misses, answered as unrecorded). The acts cursor's keyed answer at pos is `KeyedView::lookup(door, request, pos)`'s position (`keyed_hit_within_cutoff`), and an inclusive bound is caught answering from the future.	1,5,11,12	2/2
shadow-exit-code	contract	supervise::tape::shadow::{Shadow::code,ledgered,point_taint,shadow,shadow_onto}	Rests on astream claim `effects.shadow-replay`. Each decision goes in order to astream-effects' `ShadowLedger`; a row after the first that differs is tainted and that row is not; the first decision a full ledger refuses ends the feed. `Shadow::code` is `ShadowLedger::exit_code` with every decision in the ledger, else `shadow_exit_code(differs, unknown + refused.left)`, in 0..=3: 1 when a decision differs, else 3 when one was not decided again (one past the ledger included), else 0.	1,2,3,7,12	3/3

## 2. Test → spec map (TSV)

`primary` is the spec that retires the test (`§4`: it samples no spec); `also` lists the other specs it samples. Paths are relative to `crates/aterm-agent`. A conformance test's negative controls (`Buggy` knobs, old-reader reconstructions) sit under its refinement spec as that spec's mutation control. A test with no row has not been recovered (§3).

## 4. Tests that are not spec samples

| test | why | action |
|---|---|---|
| `src/harness/lights.rs::every_recorded_screen_reads_definitely_or_not_at_all` | Drift canary over a vendor fixture directory, not a universal property. | **move-to-bench** |
| `src/harness/lights.rs::every_captured_footer_screen_of_every_build_is_read` | Vendor-build drift canary over claude-<v>-footer-*.txt. | **move-to-bench** |
| `src/harness/footer_tests.rs::every_recorded_screen_has_a_rule_the_footer_can_write` | Corpus check over aterm-phase's fixtures; the general halves are footer-rule-run and footer-fit-rule-ladder. | **move-to-bench** |
| `src/harness/upgrade_drive_tests.rs::park_when_asked` | Not a test: the parked stand-in agent's body, re-executed under PARK_ENV. | **move-to-bench** |
| `src/supervise/run_engine_tests.rs::park_as_worker` | Not a test: the StandIn subprocess body. | **move-to-bench** |
| `tests/transcript_allocations.rs::transcript_readers_keep_allocation_proportional_to_input` | Allocation-budget benchmark. | **move-to-bench** |

6. Several cli and disk tests date fixtures against the real wall clock (`now_after_write`, `idle_now`), contrary to the module docs' clock-independence claim.
10. `src/harness/transcript_corpus_tests.rs::transcript_projections_answer_as_recorded`: its Err halves pin aterm_json's Display text (prose pinning).
13. Chunk 3's manifest said 332 tests; the files hold 333 `#[test]` (305+9+3+5+11). The extra is `park_when_asked`, a stand-in process body.
17. Many end-to-end upgrade_drive tests pin ledger prose as well as step words (e.g. a_drain_a_person_holds_past_its_bound_voids_the_answer_and_asks_again, a_visits_own_retarget_is_said_once_in_the_ledger); the spec is the step word and the row count.
20. `src/harness/upgrade_drive_tests.rs::the_real_classifier_conforms_to_the_upgrade_look_model`: the aterm-gui half of the bind fakes `due` from the model's `readable` bit, so the classifier rests on this test alone.
27. `src/harness/upgrade_tests.rs::every_short_argv_resumes_with_exactly_the_values_claude_parsed`: the oracle is a test-only transcription of Commander 2.1.281; it must become the Clean reference model and nothing ties it to the shipping bundle.
30. `src/harness/upgrade_status_tests.rs::the_awake_clock_is_the_machines_last_wake_or_boot` reads the live machine and partly tests a cfg(test) seam.
32. Fixed 2026-10-28: `src/harness/upgrade_queued_tests.rs` `limit_row` now stamps `now + 86_400` relative to the test's `now` rather than clamping against the historical `1_791_046_800` floor.
33. Fixed 2026-10-28: `src/supervise/answer.rs::{the_box_token_names_one_screen,a_roles_question_has_a_box_token_and_another_box_none}` assert 8-lowercase-hex format, determinism, invariance under option/focus edits outside the hashed fields, and `None` on non-question prompts without claiming FNV collision-freeness.
34. Fixed 2026-10-28: `config-reader-never-grants` states the count-key exemption (`continue_per_hour` and `human_grace_s` treat `"off"` as an unreadable non-integer that keeps its default with one note, matching `SupervisorConfig::set` and `a_value_it_cannot_read_keeps_its_default_and_only_an_off_limits`).
36. `src/supervise/limit.rs::the_local_zone_is_the_c_librarys_and_no_zone_is_made_up` depends on the runner's zone (a bound, not a value); its `date +%z` oracle is `aterm_uds::zone`'s.
40. `src/supervise/ledger_html.rs` and the HTML ledger test rely on cfg(test) checkers (is_self_contained, well_formed); the spec they imply is checked only by test code.
41. Chunk 6 (`src/supervise/policy/*`, 366 tests: turn_end_tests 212, rm_breaker_tests 47, git_config_tests 27, question_tests 23, claude_settings_tests 16, question_text_tests 12, shell_startup_tests 10, exception_tests 8, guard.rs 6, reset.rs 3, outlook_tests 2) returned no specs. Its 80 test groups are listed as `pending` rows; their statements, functions and gaps must be recovered again.
42. `src/supervise/reset_loop_tests.rs` (whole file): every screen is composed from the 2.1.304 bundle's text (2.1.287's until 2026-10-19); nothing has been seen live, so the reset specs pin a reading of the bundle.
43. Many reset_loop tests name a 'NEGATIVE CONTROL (seen by mutation)' that is not executed; each mutant must be checked against the spec explicitly.
44. `src/supervise/reset_loop_tests.rs::a_deadline_is_placed_in_the_wall_notices_zone_where_the_loop_holds_one`: without a lookup, a January date gets September's PDT offset (16:30Z instead of 17:30Z); documented as intended but wrong about wall-clock reality.
46. `src/supervise/run_engine_tests.rs::a_read_box_under_a_ticking_screen_is_approved_within_one_wake` claims latency but asserts an exact ten-request trace.
47. Several run_box_key tests replay scripts the tier1 bind already walks; they add only badge/escalated/cleared counts.
48. The reset successor tests assert ≥ 1 s real-time graces; not flaky, but tie the spec to real sleeps instead of an injected clock.
52. `src/supervise/run_tests.rs::guard_errors_are_retried_or_surfaced_never_approved`: `(2..=4).contains(&presses)` under a 1.2 s budget is timing-dependent; so are the survey settle/landing tests.
56. Chunk 9's manifest said 270 tests; its 22 files hold 278 (`tests/agent_suite.rs` holds none: its one match is in a //! comment).
60. Many turn_end_loop tests assert exact journal/badge strings; that prose goes when the spec replaces them.
61. `tests/transcript_allocations.rs`: named proportional, mostly asserts absolute caps.
63. `tests/conformance_footer_model.rs` walks only states within 5 actions of the start; the spec is over all of them.
64. Conformance negative-control tests (Buggy knobs, old-reader reconstructions) stay under their refinement spec as its mutation control, not as specs.
65. `src/supervise/turn_end_loop_tests.rs::tier1_the_real_loop_remembers_a_hidden_wall_exactly_where_the_model_does` is a Tier-1 bind living in the unit-test file instead of tests/.
